# Quantitative Trading Research

Autonomous research pipeline for discovering reproducible, net-of-cost trading edges.

## Cycle 922: The C3 `csmomentum_001` parameter-plateau gate (gate 4) on the config-faithful path — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; THE CANDIDATE STAYS REJECTED/FAILED (NO-GO)**.
- UTC 2026-10-21T05:05:00Z. Measurement cycle: **no** gate, threshold, decision mapping, universe, cost model, Sharpe convention, parameter, candidate configuration or committed artifact was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/`; the fetcher was never called; no candidate was promoted/re-fit/re-ranked; the shortlist was not re-scored.
- **The Cycle 922 hand-off's premise was stale, and the cycle did not act on it.** The hand-off said "**No backtest has been executed for C3 yet**" and asked for a first execution of the C3 baseline. That is false: the baseline was executed in **Cycle 13** (`research/run_cycle13.py` -> `research/experiments/csmomentum_baseline_wf_001.md`), the **config-faithful** month-end re-audit in **Cycle 871** (`research/reports/month_end_candidate_reaudit_001.md`) and the **pre-registered sample-policy gate table** in **Cycle 907** (`research/reports/csmomentum_common_inception_gate_001.md`), where the candidate's label is already *Rejected/Failed*. Per AGENTS.md ("Task Validation": if already complete, run verification and advance to an evidence-based next task) the cycle **verified** the committed record instead of re-executing it, and closed the **one pre-registered gate that was still open**: the **parameter-plateau gate (gate 4)**, which every config-faithful record had reported as *NOT EVALUATED* because the 18-configuration pre-registered grid had never been evaluated on that path.
- **What the cycle measured.** New read-only tool `research/csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval.py` (report `research/reports/csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval_001.md`, record `research/experiments/csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval_001.md`) evaluates the frozen baseline (252/21/0.5) **and** the whole pre-registered grid (3 lookbacks x 2 skips x 3 top fractions = **18 unique** configurations) on the **config-faithful month-end path** (`src/backtest/rebalance.py:build_month_end_weights`, 1-day signal lag, 3 bps round trip, the frozen `config/csmomentum_candidate_001.yaml` splits and 10y/12m/6m walk-forward with 21d purge + embargo, fixed parameters across folds). The price panel is read **directly from the committed parquet cache** (`data/raw/*.parquet`); the fetcher is never called and nothing is downloaded or written.
- **Reproduction anchor (fail closed).** The tool reproduces the published config-faithful record before it publishes anything: union sample 48 folds, WF mean OOS Sharpe **0.697524**, median **0.617881**, positive **79.17%**, pooled **400** trades; path Sharpe **0.4247** backtest-scale / **0.6051** canonical, max DD **-0.4474**, turnover **2.16**, 232 trade days, 7.56 mean positions; and the pre-registered common-inception sample 21 folds, mean **0.586081**, median **0.631134**, positive **76.19%**, pooled **166** trades. All 16 anchor comparisons MATCH at the published decimals.
- **The plateau gate (gate 4) is now EVALUATED: 3 of 3 lookbacks within tolerance -> PASS.** Baseline test Sharpe **0.4878**; tolerance **0.1000** (`max(0.1, 10% x |baseline|)`, the repository convention); lookback 189 -> 0.5186 (|d| 0.0308), 252 (baseline) -> 0.4878, 315 -> 0.4656 (|d| 0.0222) — all three within tolerance. The measurement is **invariant to the sample definition** (the test split lies entirely after the 252+21 warm-up ramp), so the union sample and the pre-registered common-inception slice measure the identical gate.
- **The verdict is unchanged and the rejection is robust.** Gate 1 (WF mean OOS Sharpe **0.697524** < **0.9**) FAILs on the union sample and on the pre-registered common-inception sample (**0.586081**); gate 2 (positive fold rate 0.791667 >= 0.5) PASSes; gate 3 (pooled OOS trades 400 > 100) PASSes; gate 4 (plateau) PASSes. The pre-registered falsification condition (gate 1 **or** gate 2 fails) is met -> **Decision NO-GO, research label Rejected/Failed** (unchanged). **0 of the 18 pre-registered configurations clear gate 1** (the best reaches a WF mean OOS Sharpe below 0.9), so the rejection is **not** an artefact of the pre-registered baseline — the whole pre-registered neighbourhood fails.
- **Multiple-testing control, with the scale disclosed.** DSR at the pre-registered trial count N=27: **0.1248** (DSR probability 0.5496; observed test Sharpe 0.6635, deflation threshold 0.6215, SE 0.3373, E[max of 27] 1.8422); at the actual N=18: **0.3098** (0.6216); PSR vs SR=0 **0.9754**, vs the 0.9 gate **0.2417**; block-bootstrap 95% CI [0.0248, 1.3435]. **Scale disclosure:** the DSR/PSR here use the repository's *current* canonical Sharpe convention (`arithmetic_annualized_rf0`, unified in Cycle 861), so they are **not comparable** with the committed Cycle 13 record's DSR (**-0.6484**), which was computed with the pre-Cycle-861 estimator. Reconstructed with the record's own estimator and the same bootstrap SE, this path's DSR is **-0.3961** at N=27 and **-0.2111** at N=18 — negative, i.e. on the record's own scale the observed test Sharpe does not survive the deflation either. The DSR is not part of the falsification condition and does not enter the verdict. (The pre-registration's label of "27 configurations" is a mislabel — 3 x 2 x 3 yields 18 unique combinations; it is disclosed, not silently corrected, and no configuration was invented to reach 27.)
- **A measured disclosure about the selection breadth.** The shared selection module (`src/features/momentum.py:select_top_decile`) holds every asset whose cross-sectional **rank** is >= max_rank x (1 - top_fraction) — the rule documented in `research/hypotheses/sectorrotation_001.md` — and the boundary rank is inclusive, so at 18 assets and top_fraction 0.5 it holds the top **10 of 18 (55.6%)** on every test-window session, one asset more than the literal "top 50%" reading of the csmomentum hypothesis prose (9 of 18). The rule is identical for the baseline and for all 18 grid configurations, so it does not bias the plateau comparison. (An intermediate draft of this cycle's report mis-described the module as a momentum-*value* threshold; it was corrected before publication and a regression test now pins the correct rule.)
- **Threshold-reading disclosure.** The hypothesis states the plateau gate in the count form (">= 2 of 3 lookbacks"), which is what the tool applies; the frozen config expresses the same threshold as a fraction (`plateau_fraction_min: 0.667`), and because 2/3 = 0.6667 rounds to 0.667, a literal `>=` on the rounded fraction would demand 3 of 3. Both readings are published and they agree here (3 of 3, fraction 1.0000).
- **Checks and artifacts.** The tool runs **16 structural checks** (frozen config consumed as-is; the pre-registered grid literals still declared in the frozen config; the pre-registered trial count read from the config; the cost model matches the frozen config; the evaluation window ends at the frozen test end; the union path / union walk-forward / common-inception walk-forward anchors reproduced; the plateau measurement invariant to the sample; the selection mask equal to the documented rank rule; `data/`+`config/` byte-identical; the committed C3 artifacts byte-identical; the fetcher never called; the baseline path built exactly once and reused by every fold; the grid size 18; the plateau threshold reading disclosed) — all PASS, exit 0. The read-only guarantee is enforced by SHA-256 fingerprinting every file under `data/` and `config/` and every committed C3 artifact before and after the run.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval.py` with **24 tests** — the tool runs with `--no-report`, exits 0, prints `STRUCTURAL: PASS` / `DECISION|NO-GO` / `LABEL|Rejected/Failed` / `PLATEAU|3|3|` / `ROBUSTNESS_CLEARING_GATE1|0|18` and leaves the tracked records byte-identical; bit-for-bit determinism across two runs; `data/`+`config/` byte-identical; the frozen config and `config/csmomentum_candidate_001.yaml` unmodified; no fetcher call; the baseline is consumed as frozen (252/21/0.5) with the gate thresholds read from the config; the three published anchors are reproduced; the plateau gate is evaluated and passes (3 of 3) and the failed gate is exactly `wf_mean_oos_sharpe`; no grid configuration clears gate 1; the walk-forward uses one fixed path; the grid is exactly the 18 pre-registered combinations; the selection follows the documented rank rule and the report does not claim a value threshold; the new records carry the verdict.
- **Verification:** `python research/csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval.py` -> `STRUCTURAL: PASS`, `DECISION|NO-GO`, `LABEL|Rejected/Failed`, exit 0; `python -m pytest -q tests/test_csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval.py` -> **24 passed**; `python -m pytest -q` -> **451 passed**, 3 warnings (427 pre-existing + the 24 new); `git status --porcelain data/ config/` -> empty; `git status --porcelain` -> only the four new Cycle 922 files.
- **Decision unchanged:** the C3 candidate is **rejected/failed**; its last open pre-registered gate is now evaluated and its rejection is shown to be robust across the whole pre-registered parameter neighbourhood; the incumbent's published verdict and the live-deployment stance are unchanged. **Handoff:** evidence in `research/reports/csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval_001.md` and `research/experiments/csmomentum_plateau_gate_eval_001.md`; `.kilo-runtime/worker-disposition.json` updated. **Next task (Cycle 923):** the incumbent's published walk-forward PASS rests on a +0.0473 margin that is **not phase-stable** (phase A mean 1.385035 vs phase B mean 0.509580 -> gate 1 FAIL under phase B alone, Cycle 910/911) and that weakness is still **unexplained** — measure, with pre-specified observable state buckets, whether the phase-B weakness is an identifiable market state that could be *monitored* on the live stance or a calendar/protocol artefact that no observable state can flag, without changing the published protocol, the gates or the published record.

## Cycle 921: The `tsmomentum_sma175_candidate` full promotion gate verdict — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; REJECTED/FAILED (NO-GO)**.
- UTC 2026-10-21T04:11:00Z. Measurement/verification cycle: **no** gate, threshold, decision mapping, universe, cost model, Sharpe convention, parameter, candidate configuration or committed artifact outside the two Cycle 920 records was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/`; the incumbent's published record is unchanged; no candidate promoted/re-fit/re-ranked.
- **The verdict.** `research/tsmomentum_sma175_promotion_gate_eval.py` (read-only, deterministic) evaluates the SMA-175 variant against the full gate suite of `config/promotion_gates.yaml` over 8,488 sessions: `track_record_length` 8488 PASS, `full_period_sharpe` **1.607869** PASS, `max_drawdown` -0.094633 PASS, `rolling_63d_max_dd` -0.038564 PASS, `regime_flips` 277 PASS, `rolling_63d_sharpe` **-0.448918 FAIL**, `psr` **0.896592 FAIL** -> **Decision: NO-GO**, `CANDIDATE_PROMOTABLE: NO`. Two gates fail independently of each other, so the variant is **rejected/failed** and is not a live alternative to the incumbent; the incumbent's own verdict and the live-deployment stance are unchanged.
- **Defect 1 found and corrected — the `psr` gate was computed against the candidate's own Sharpe.** The Cycle 920 tool passed `sr_target=backtest_sharpe_ratio(net_ret)` (the candidate's own backtest-scale Sharpe) into `compute_psr`, i.e. it scored the candidate against itself, and published 0.856637 — a value not comparable with any other PSR in the repository. `config/promotion_gates.yaml` defines that gate against the **fixed** backtest-baseline Sharpe **1.4178** ("of the paper return series against the backtest Sharpe baseline 1.4178"), which is what `src/simulation/evaluation.py` (`GoNoGoConfig.backtest_sharpe = 1.4178`) and every other PSR call in the repository use (`research/psr_feasibility.py`, `research/rebalance_schedule_audit.py`, `research/promotion_feasibility_backtest.py`, `research/common_inception_sample_audit.py`). The corrected PSR is **0.896592** (target 1.4178, `n_bootstrap=1000`, `block_size=22`, `seed=20240101` — the repository's own bootstrap settings), the target is now a module constant whose source is published in both records, and the residual scale difference is disclosed in the report (canonical Sharpe 1.607869 vs backtest-baseline-scale diagnostic 1.447467 vs the un-restated 1.4178 reference, the Cycle 861 convention). **The correction does not rescue the candidate**: 0.896592 < 0.95, and the verdict is unchanged because `rolling_63d_sharpe` fails independently of the PSR.
- **Defect 2 corrected — `OVERALL` is the tool-check verdict, not the candidate verdict.** One intermediate revision set `OVERALL` to "structural checks pass AND no gate fails", which printed `OVERALL: FAIL` next to `Decision: NO-GO` and made a correctly executed evaluation exit 1. That is not this repository's convention: `OVERALL` is the verdict on the tool's own internal checks in every other read-only evaluation tool — `research/rolling_63d_gate_refresh.py` prints `OVERALL: PASS` while one of its own checks is "decision is NO-GO on the committed record", and `research/sectorrotation_long_sample_feasibility.py` published `OVERALL: PASS` with a NO-GO decision (README Cycle 869) — and it is what the Cycle 920 acceptance criterion 2 requires ("`OVERALL: PASS` only if all internal structural checks pass; fails closed otherwise"). `OVERALL` is therefore restored to the structural verdict, **the pre-registration was not edited to fit the code** (criterion 2 is preserved verbatim at commit `c3a22b6`), and the two axes are now reported separately and labelled in both records: `STRUCTURAL: PASS` / `OVERALL: PASS` (the tool's own checks) / `DECISION: NO-GO` / `CANDIDATE_PROMOTABLE: NO` (the candidate).
- **The test no longer mutates a tracked artifact.** The new regression test ran the tool through `subprocess` with no output redirection, so every suite run re-stamped the two committed records with the current date and commit hash. The tool now supports `--no-report` (the convention already used by `research/gate_false_negative_rate.py` and `research/long_sample_overlay_attribution.py`), the test runs it that way, and the test asserts the SHA-256 of both tracked records is **unchanged** across the run. The suite's other, pre-existing side effect (re-stamping `research/reports/sectorrotation_long_sample_feasibility_001.md` and `research/reports/tsmomentum_sma175_candidate_eval_001.md` with the current commit hash) is reverted after the run, exactly as every prior cycle did.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_tsmomentum_sma175_promotion_gate_eval.py` with **14 tests** — the PSR target is the documented 1.4178 and the tool contains no candidate-specific target; the tool runs with `--no-report`, exits 0, prints `STRUCTURAL`/`OVERALL`/`DECISION`/`CANDIDATE_PROMOTABLE`/`PSR_TARGET` and leaves both tracked records byte-identical; the reported PSR equals an independent recomputation from the raw parquet cache and equals 0.896592; `OVERALL` is pinned to the tool-check verdict while the candidate verdict is pinned to NO-GO with failed gates `['rolling_63d_sharpe', 'psr']`; both records state the two axes separately; the committed records carry the corrected PSR and no longer carry 0.856637; `data/`+`config/` unchanged; `research/run_cycle6.py` and `config/promotion_gates.yaml` unmodified.
- **Verification:** `python research/tsmomentum_sma175_promotion_gate_eval.py` -> `STRUCTURAL: PASS`, `OVERALL: PASS`, `DECISION|NO-GO`, `CANDIDATE_PROMOTABLE|NO`, `PSR_TARGET|1.4178`, exit 0; `python -m pytest -q tests/test_tsmomentum_sma175_promotion_gate_eval.py` -> **14 passed in 5.12s**; `python -m pytest -v --tb=short` -> **427 passed**, 3 warnings in 583.64s (0:09:43) — 413 pre-existing + the 14 new; `git status --porcelain data/ config/` -> empty; `git diff --stat -- research/run_cycle6.py` -> empty; `git diff --name-only HEAD -- config/promotion_gates.yaml` -> empty. The suite was run as a tracked background process, not blocked on: it takes about ten minutes and the invocation watchdog terminates a worker whose child is silent for 600 s.
- **Handoff:** evidence in `research/reports/tsmomentum_sma175_promotion_gate_eval_001.md` and `research/experiments/tsmomentum_sma175_promotion_gate_eval_001.md`; `.kilo-runtime/worker-disposition.json` updated. **Decision of this cycle: `tsmomentum_sma175_candidate` is rejected/failed (NO-GO on `rolling_63d_sharpe` and `psr`); the incumbent's verdict and the live-deployment stance are unchanged.** Next task (Cycle 921): publish the decision itself — label the candidate in the shortlist and quantify the reachability of its two failed gates (the observed Sharpe required for `psr` = 0.95 at the published Sharpe SE 0.150565, and the additional sessions at the record's observed mean that would be needed), so that "rejected" is a decision with numbers behind it rather than a bare gate table.

## Cycle 910: `tsmomentum_spytrend_001` walk-forward under non-overlapping (embargoed) fold design — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; SENSITIVITY ONLY (verdict unchanged)**.
- UTC 2026-10-20T16:22:00Z. Measurement/sensitivity cycle: **no** gate, threshold, decision mapping, universe, cost model, Sharpe convention, parameter, candidate configuration or committed artifact was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/`; `src/evaluation/walkforward.py` not changed; published protocol unchanged; no candidate promoted/re-fit/re-ranked.
- **What the cycle measured.** New read-only tool `research/tsmomentum_walkforward_embargo_audit.py` (report `research/reports/tsmomentum_walkforward_embargo_001.md`, record `research/experiments/tsmomentum_walkforward_embargo_001.md`) evaluates three fold designs over the **same continuously computed point-in-time path** as the published Cycle 6 record: the committed candidate definition and overlay are consumed as-is from `research/run_cycle6.py` (226-day TSMOM, top_fraction 0.5, vol-target 10% + drawdown control 15%, SPY 200-day SMA trend filter, 3 bps round-trip, 10y/12m/6m walk-forward with 21d purge/embargo) and the price panel is read **directly from the committed parquet cache** (`data/raw/`) — the fetcher is never called and nothing is downloaded or written. The path is computed once and shared by all three designs (the published protocol's own per-fold evaluation recomputes it 96 times), so the whole audit runs in ~4 s.
- **Reproduction anchor (published design):** 48 folds, WF mean OOS Sharpe **0.9473**, median 1.2933, positive 91.7%, pooled 6013 trades, 125.3 trades/fold — the published record is resolved **by content** (the committed file that carries the published aggregate table and fold table, not a hard-coded name) and **all 11 aggregate metrics and all 48 published folds are reproduced** (test windows, train/test OOS Sharpe to the published decimal, trade counts) -> anchor **MATCH**. The tool fails closed if it does not.
- **Design A (non-overlapping test windows, primary):** step = test window length (12m) so consecutive test windows share no session: **24 folds**, overlap **0.0 days**, disjoint count **== fold count** (24); WF mean OOS Sharpe **1.385035** (median 1.420426, positive 91.67%, pooled 2952). The published gate-1 outcome **survives** and the margin **grows**: mean 0.9473 -> 1.3850, gate-1 margin (>= 0.9) **+0.0473 -> +0.4850**, all three evaluated gates still pass.
- **Design B (embargo applied literally to fold construction):** next training window starts after previous test end + 21d embargo; yields only **3 folds** (too few for a stable gate table) and is published as informational with no forced gate table.
- **Independence:** the published design's 48 folds overlap by **175.0 days** on average and carry only **24** pairwise-disjoint test windows; Design A has **0 overlap** and disjoint count equal to fold count; Design B 3 folds / 3 disjoint.
- **Verdict:** the incumbent's published PASS is **not** an artefact of fold overlap — removing the overlap *raises* the measured mean and widens the margin, so the published gate outcome is unchanged. No gates/thresholds/universe/cost model/parameters changed; the SMA-plateau gate is not re-evaluated (it is not fold-dependent).
- **Two findings the handoff's premise did not anticipate, published rather than smoothed over.**
  1. **The published 48-fold mean is the average of two disjoint 24-fold sub-designs whose means differ by 0.88 Sharpe.** The generator steps the folds by 6 months over a 12-month test window, so the published folds are phase A (folds 1, 3, 5, ...; mean **1.385035** — this is Design A) and phase B (folds 2, 4, 6, ...; mean **0.509580**). Under phase B alone the gate-1 outcome would be **FAIL** (0.5096 < 0.9), so the published +0.0473 margin is not a phase-stable property of the protocol.
  2. **The published mean is dominated by two thin-trade folds.** Fold 12 (test 2008-01-22..2009-01-07, **2 trades**, OOS Sharpe **-21.8299**) and fold 13 (**8 trades**, -0.2665) are the only folds with fewer than ten trades. Leave-one-out on the published 48 folds: dropping fold 12 moves the mean from 0.947307 to **1.431928** (+0.4846), dropping fold 13 to 0.973441 (+0.0261). The trimmed mean (min and max removed) is 1.378012 and the published median 1.293299, so the published 0.9473 sits **0.44 below the median of its own fold set**.
- **Checks and artifacts:** the tool runs **10 structural checks** (published record resolved by content; cache-only path built; published aggregate reproduced; published 48-fold table reproduced; published design has test-window overlap; Design A windows pairwise disjoint; gate thresholds consumed from the published record (0.9 / 0.5); `data/` unchanged; `config/` unchanged; committed artifacts unchanged) — all PASS, exit 0. The read-only guarantee is enforced by hashing every file under `data/` and `config/` and every git-tracked artifact under `src/`, `config/`, `research/experiments/`, `research/reports/` before and after the run.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_tsmomentum_walkforward_embargo_audit.py` with **11 tests** — the tool's OVERALL, both artifacts exist, the anchor (48 folds, 0.9473, 6013 pooled trades) and the per-fold reproduction, the published record resolved by content to `research/experiments/tsmomentum_regime_filter_wf_001.md`, Design A windows pairwise disjoint vs the published design's overlap > 0, `data/`+`config/` byte-identical before and after, committed artifacts byte-identical before and after, bit-for-bit determinism across two runs, and three fail-closed cases (malformed published record, missing cached prices, missing experiments directory).
- **Verification:** `python research/tsmomentum_walkforward_embargo_audit.py` -> `OVERALL: PASS`, exit 0 (published 48 folds / 0.9473; Design A 24 folds / 1.3850; Design B 3 folds; gate-1 outcome changes: NO); `python -m pytest -q tests/test_tsmomentum_walkforward_embargo_audit.py` -> **11 passed in 28.73s**; `python -m pytest -q` -> **310 passed**, 3 warnings in 601.77s, the same **310 passed** in 598.42s against the committed tree (`03aeee2`) and **310 passed**, 3 warnings in 591.40s against the final tree (`eab6b4d`); `git status --porcelain data/` -> empty; `git diff --stat src/ config/ research/experiments/ research/reports/` -> empty (the two new artifacts are the only new files; no committed artifact is modified). Running the suite re-stamps `research/reports/sectorrotation_long_sample_feasibility_001.md` with the current commit hash (a pre-existing side effect of that report's generator); that one-line stamp is reverted so the audited trees stay clean.
- **Environment note (it cost three worker invocations):** the worker image ships **without the repository's Python dependencies** — `pandas`, `pyarrow`, `pyyaml`, `pytest`, `yfinance`, `scipy` and (for the full suite) `arch`, `statsmodels`, `ccxt` must be installed from `requirements.txt` before any tool or test can run (`python3 -m pip install -r requirements.txt`; no repository file is touched by it). The full suite takes about ten minutes and the invocation watchdog terminates a worker whose child is silent for 600 s, so the suite must be run in the background and polled, not blocked on.
- **Decision unchanged:** no candidate was executed, re-tuned, re-ranked or promoted; the incumbent's published verdict (**Credible Candidate**) and all three of its published validation gates stand unchanged; `csmomentum_001` and `sectorrotation_001` stay **Rejected/Failed**. **Handoff:** NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md replaced with Cycle 911 — measure how much of the incumbent's published walk-forward **mean** depends on the fold phase and on the two thin-trade folds (leave-one-out influence, trimmed mean, phase decomposition), and state whether the published PASS is stable, without changing the published protocol, the gates or the published record.

## Cycle 908: `csmomentum_001` walk-forward under non-overlapping (embargoed) fold design — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; SENSITIVITY ONLY (verdict unchanged)**.
- UTC 2026-10-20T14:10:00Z. Measurement/sensitivity cycle: **no** gate, threshold, decision mapping, universe, cost model, Sharpe convention, parameter, candidate configuration or committed artifact was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/`; `src/evaluation/walkforward.py` not changed; published protocol unchanged; no candidate promoted/re-fit/re-ranked.

## Cycle 907: `csmomentum_001`'s gate table re-derived on the three pre-registered samples — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; MEASUREMENT ONLY (the verdict does not move)**.

  | `common_inception_window` | 5,202 | 2006-02-06..2026-10-09 | 21 | **0.5861** | 0.6311 | 76.19% | 166 | FAIL | PASS | PASS | NOT EVALUATED | **Rejected/Failed** |
- **The reproduction anchor.** The union sample reproduces the committed config-faithful record of `research/reports/month_end_candidate_reaudit_001.md` (section 2.3) at the published decimals — 48 folds, mean 0.6975243375140185 (published 0.6975), canonical 0.8403888341999609 (0.8404), median 0.6178806353181572 (0.6179), positive 0.7916666666666666 (0.7917), pooled 400 — and the tool's OVERALL **fails** if it does not. The two values the path must not produce are not produced: the legacy calendar path (0.6868) and the daily re-rank path (0.6928).
- **The question the cycle was asked — answered: the published rejection is NOT an artefact of the sample definition.** On the pre-registered common-inception samples the walk-forward mean OOS Sharpe is **0.5861**, i.e. *below* the union sample's 0.6975, so the gate-1 margin is **larger** on the pre-registered sample (0.3139 vs 0.2025), not smaller. The candidate stays **Rejected/Failed** on all three samples; the trade-count screen still passes there (166 > 100), so the rejection is a return-quality rejection, not a statistical-power one.
- **Two findings that the handoff's premise did not anticipate, and that are published rather than smoothed over.**
  1. **The "earliest folds are trained on a one-asset cross-section" reading is correct about the *training* window only.** The union sample's 1,763 degenerate sessions (fewer than 5 selectable assets) run from 1993-01-29 to **2000-01-21** and therefore lie entirely **before** the earliest fold's test window (2002-09-30, when 12 assets already carry a valid momentum). The pre-registered diagnostic (breadth at the fold's **training-window end**) is not degenerate for any fold of any sample (a ten-year training window is longer than the ragged era: the union's fold 1 ends its training window on 2002-08-29 with 12 valid assets). 15 of the union sample's 48 folds contain a degenerate stretch *inside* their training window; **no** fold of **any** sample trades a cross-section narrower than 5 assets. The published gate table was therefore not produced by a one-asset, time-series regime.
  2. **The walk-forward's folds are not independent observations.** The pre-registered design steps the folds by 6 months over a 12-month test window, so consecutive test windows overlap by ~175 days, and the repository's generator (`src/evaluation/walkforward.py:generate_walkforward_splits`) **accepts but never applies** the configured 21-day `embargo_days` (the next fold's rolling training window contains the tail of the previous fold's test window). The honest count of disjoint test windows is **24** (union) and **11** (common-inception), not 48 and 21. This is a property of the published protocol shared by every published walk-forward record in the repository; it is published, not fixed, because fixing it would change the published protocol.
- **The slice and the window give identical gate tables, and the reason is published.** The slice's signal has no history before the common inception, so it is flat for the first 273 sessions (lookback 252 + skip 21; first selectable asset 2007-03-09), while the window's signal is live on its first session; the two paths coincide from the first month-end signal both frames can form, and the common-inception samples' first out-of-sample fold starts in 2015-11 — years after that. A **matched-fold-window** sensitivity (all three paths on the same 20 test windows) returns identical numbers (mean 0.7108, median 0.5872, 80.00%, 160 pooled trades), which shows that the residual difference between the samples' own-calendar means (0.1114 Sharpe) is a fold-calendar effect of about **one standard error** of the fold mean (fold-Sharpe std 0.7784 over 48 folds) and is not resolvable at 20-48 folds.
- **Checks and artifacts:** the tool runs **16 structural checks** (policy universe matches the frozen candidate config; the common inception is re-derived from `data/raw` as the max of the per-symbol first dates; the aligned price index equals the raw-cache union index; the union sample reproduces the committed config-faithful record; the legacy/daily values are not produced; the config-faithful path equals `src/backtest/rebalance.py:build_month_end_weights`; every segment is the selection of its own signal date and is effective strictly after it; the window ends at the frozen `evaluation.splits.test_end`; the six cached sessions 2026-10-12..2026-10-19 are excluded; the common-inception sample is fully populated and the union sample is ragged; the union sample's degenerate sessions precede the earliest test window; the plateau gate is NOT EVALUATED; every sample publishes a decision; `data/`+`config/` unchanged; the four committed records unchanged) — all PASS, exit 0. The tool reads the committed parquet cache directly and never calls the fetcher, so it cannot download or write.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_csmomentum_sample_gate_audit.py` with **20 tests** — 8 fail-closed policy variants plus a missing-policy case and a well-formed policy that names a different universe, the tool's OVERALL, `data/`+`config/` byte-identical before and after, the four committed records byte-identical before and after, the common inception re-derived independently from the raw cache (with the per-symbol SHA-256 checked against the cache), the reproduction anchor, the forbidden legacy/daily values, the three gate tables and their decisions, the frozen test end, and **bit-for-bit determinism across two runs**.
- **Verification:** `python research/csmomentum_sample_gate_audit.py` -> `OVERALL: PASS`, 16/16 checks, exit 0; `python -m pytest -q tests/test_csmomentum_sample_gate_audit.py` -> **20 passed in 75.87s**; `python -m pytest -q` -> **284 passed**, 3 warnings in 473.89s; `git status --porcelain data/ config/` -> empty.
- **Decision unchanged:** no candidate was executed, re-tuned, re-ranked or promoted; `csmomentum_001` stays **Rejected/Failed** on its own pre-registered gates on all three pre-registered samples; the incumbent stays **NO-GO**; the plateau gate stays **NOT EVALUATED** (the 27-configuration grid is out of scope). **Handoff:** NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md replaced with Cycle 908 — measure the candidate's walk-forward under a **non-overlapping (embargoed) fold design** (the protocol question this cycle surfaced) and publish the delta against the published protocol, without changing the published protocol.

All four gaps are closed here; the policy itself is **unchanged** (a new key would have been a silent re-pre-registration).
  1. **The policy validation was too weak to fail closed.** The tool accepted a candidate section that carried only `rule_id`. It now validates the whole pre-registration — universe (non-empty list of symbols), `rule_id`, `derivation.rule`, `long_sample_diagnostic.evaluation_sample == common_inception_slice`, `publish_alongside` containing both `common_inception_window` and `union_sample`, `warm_up_convention.slice` / `.window_preserving`, a non-empty `evidence_note`, a non-empty `conditions_for_revisit` — **and** refuses a section that carries a frozen date (`common_inception`, `inception_date`, `start_date`, `sample_start`), because the rule must be re-derived, never copied.

The first version of the check compared `breadth_max` (18 in both variants, so it could never pass) and was corrected to compare `opening_breadth` — the check that actually discriminates the two samples.
  3. **The union sample's selectable breadth was published under the wrong label.** The `union_sample` block carried the *window's* breadth (5,208 sessions) instead of the union index's own (8,488 sessions). Fixed: the union sample now publishes its own breadth — **273** sessions with zero selectable assets, **1,763 of 8,488 (20.8%) with fewer than five** (the `select_top_decile` single-asset fallback regime), first selectable asset 1994-02-28 — and the common-inception slice publishes 273 such sessions, the warm-up-preserving window none. This is the number the next cycle needs: on the union sample a fifth of the sessions are not a cross-section at all.
  4. **The read-only claim is now enforced, not assumed.** The aligned price frame is built straight from the cached parquet files (the same files `YFinanceFetcher` reads with `force=False`), so the audit can never download or write; a `data_and_config_unchanged` check (SHA-256 fingerprint of every file under `data/` and `config/` before and after the run) is part of the tool's `OVERALL`. The per-symbol **cache fingerprint** (rows, first/last date, SHA-256) is published and derived at run time.

Common-inception slice 2006-02-06..2026-10-19, 5,208 sessions, price width **18 on every session** (fully populated), 273 sessions without a selectable asset. Warm-up-preserving window: same 5,208 sessions, 0 sessions without a selectable asset.

- **The gap closed.** `research/promotion_feasibility_backtest.py` published `sample.start_date: 2004-11-18` (the *common* inception, the max of the per-symbol first dates) next to `session_count: 8488` (the length of the **union calendar** from 1993-01-29) and printed `SAMPLE: 2004-11-18..2026-10-19 (8488 sessions)` — a label and a count that described two different samples. The `sample` block now publishes the **label** (`label_start_date`, `label_end_date`) and the **realized** window (`realized_start_date`, `realized_end_date`, `realized_session_count`) side by side; the historical `start_date` / `end_date` / `session_count` keys now carry the realized window, so no key describes a sample that was not computed, and the console line reads `SAMPLE: realized 1993-01-29..2026-10-19 (8488 sessions), label 2004-11-18..2026-10-19, universe=[...]`.
- **The realized window is derived independently, not copied from the engine.** The tool reads every `data/raw/*.parquet` once, builds the sorted union of the per-symbol indices, and checks that the backtest's own return index **equals** that union (`realized window equals raw-cache union index: True`). The ragged edge of the union is published with it: per-symbol first date, last date and row count, plus a **cache fingerprint** (rows, first/last date and the file's SHA-256 per symbol) that is *derived at run time* — a first draft that hard-coded `SPY 8488 rows / 2026-10-19 / sha256 971d02284b9790df…` was rejected, because a frozen fingerprint is exactly the stale literal the fingerprint exists to prevent: a later append has to appear as a fingerprint change, not as an unexplained number change.

Both anchors are now *checked on every run*: the incumbent path truncated at 2026-10-16 (8,487 sessions) must reproduce Sharpe 0.5646255767978904 / max DD -0.6224126346564325 / PSR 2.2219753177036448e-08, and the first 449 sessions of the paper record must reproduce the canonical Sharpe 1.748401 (`reference check [incumbent_reference_sample]: PASS`, `reference check [paper_record_reference_sample]: PASS`); the tool's `OVERALL` is `PASS` only if those checks and the realized-window check hold.

## Cycle 901: Append-Proof Verification Invariants — the 2026-10-19 session append had turned three evidence tools and two tests red — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; TOOLING/VERIFICATION ONLY**.
- UTC 2026-10-20T10:51:00Z. Verification-hygiene cycle: **no** candidate, universe, cost model, gate, threshold, configuration, state, metric or deployment decision was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/`.
- **The gap closed.** Cycle 900's data refresh appended one completed session (2026-10-19) to the raw cache and the paper record. Every invariant that was pinned to the *length* of a record went stale with it and reported a failure that had nothing to do with the gates: `research/rolling_63d_gate_refresh.py` (exited 1, `OVERALL: FAIL`, 10/11 checks — the canonical Sharpe of the 450-session record is 1.771084, the check demanded 1.748401), `research/rebalance_schedule_audit.py` (exited 1, `OVERALL: FAIL` — the incumbent's Sharpe/PSR are the numbers of the 8,487-session sample), `research/long_sample_overlay_attribution.py` (exited 1 — the committed artifact was not "reproduced" and the Cycle 862 continuity check compared against a moved window), plus `tests/test_rebalance_schedule.py` and `tests/test_rolling_63d_gate_refresh.py`. A false failure in a read-only evidence tool hides the gate results it exists to publish and makes the suite red, so real regressions stop being visible.
- **The delta was classified before anything was edited.** Against `HEAD~1`: the paper record is append-only (449 -> 450 sessions, first 449 `cumulative_returns`/`cumulative_dates` identical, one session appended 2026-10-19 +0.433912%, `peak_nav` unchanged); all eight raw parquet files gained exactly one row (2026-10-19), **zero dropped dates**, every historical row bit-identical (`atol=0, rtol=0`; only the `Volume` dtype widened int64->float64 because the appended row is float). Nothing in the history was rewritten.
- **The proof that nothing but the data moved.** Truncating the incumbent path at 2026-10-16 (8,487 sessions) reproduces the published artifact **exactly**: Sharpe 0.5646255767978904, max DD -0.6224126346564325, PSR 2.2219753177036448e-08; the first 449 sessions of the paper record reproduce the documented canonical Sharpe 1.748401; the 63-session Cycle 862 window (2026-07-21..2026-10-16) reproduces the published decomposition to 4.587e-07 pp. The full-sample numbers moved only because the sample grew: incumbent Sharpe 0.5646255767978904 -> 0.5657179297830458, PSR 2.2219753177036448e-08 -> 1.6549061233035153e-08, paper-record Sharpe 1.748401 -> 1.771084.
- **The fix: assert on a reference sample, publish the current sample.** The repository already had this convention for the long-sample artifact (`PUBLISHED_BACKTEST` with `label_start_date`/`label_end_date`); it is now applied to every record-length-specific invariant. Each pinned metric is asserted on the committed data truncated at a fixed date (state: the first 449 sessions; incumbent: the 8,487 sessions through 2026-10-16; Cycle 862: the 63 sessions through 2026-10-16). Because the engine's path is point-in-time (the weights at t use data up to t only) and the state's prefix is immutable, truncating reproduces the published path exactly: an append can no longer invalidate an invariant, while a rewrite of history still fails it. The current-sample numbers are still published (the committed report is the snapshot, regenerated byte-identically, and it is the data-change alarm), the raw-cache fingerprint (per-symbol rows, last date, SHA-256) is printed next to them, and the gate tool no longer compares against any frozen constant at all — it re-derives the published Sharpe from the record with the canonical formula written out and checks it against the production gate value.
- **What did not change.** Paper record: decision **NO-GO**, failed gates `['rolling_63d_sharpe', 'psr']`, rolling 63d Sharpe -0.448918, PSR 0.695606. Candidate backtest: decision **NO-GO**, failed gates `['full_period_sharpe', 'rolling_63d_sharpe', 'max_drawdown', 'psr']`. No threshold, cost model, universe, candidate configuration, Sharpe convention or committed metric was touched; the state file and the raw cache are byte-identical before and after every tool run.
- **Artifacts:** report `research/reports/reference_sample_invariance_001.md`; changed `research/rolling_63d_gate_refresh.py`, `research/rebalance_schedule_audit.py`, `research/long_sample_overlay_attribution.py`, `research/trailing_window_attribution.py`, `tests/test_rebalance_schedule.py` (one new append-proof test); regenerated `research/reports/rebalance_schedule_001.md` and `research/reports/long_sample_overlay_attribution_001.md` (+ `.json`).

## Cycle 884: Roll-Date Visibility in the Cached Futures Series — shortlist rank 7 stays blocked, but on a **price-only** block — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; DIAGNOSTIC**.
- UTC 2026-10-19T23:55:00Z. Diagnostic only: no candidate, universe, cost model, gate, threshold, configuration, state or deployment decision was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/` (1,848 files fingerprinted before and after the run and compared).
- **The gap closed.** Cycle 883 recorded shortlist rank 7 (`futuresbasis_001`) as **Blocked by data/infrastructure** with the claim that the roll *dates* are not detectable from the cached continuous front-month series ("no clean monthly roll signature"). **That claim was wrong.** The cached series carry a `Volume` column that is the **front contract's** volume; at a roll the contract behind the series changes, so the expiring contract's volume run-off followed by the new front's volume recovery is a deterministic, point-in-time signature. `research/futures_roll_visibility.py` (read-only, offline, exit 0 with `OVERALL: PASS`) detects **127 / 125 / 72 / 62** roll events for CL-F / NG-F / GC-F / ZB-F with modal spacings of **29 / 30 / 63 / 91 days** — matching each product's monthly, bi-monthly and quarterly contract cycle, which a noise-driven detector could not reproduce.
- **Independent verification without per-contract data.** Yahoo's per-contract volume series for the currently listed Nov-2026 and Dec-2026 contracts show the cached chain's volume switching identity exactly at the roll: 222,965 (= the Nov-2026 contract) through 2026-10-16, 1,724 (= the Dec-2026 contract) from 2026-10-19, and the per-contract price series equals the chain over 2026-09-23..2026-10-16. The Oct-2026 contract was therefore the front month up to 2026-09-22 and the **roll date is 2026-09-23** — the event the volume rule finds in the committed cache, from the cache alone. The detector's recall is imperfect (it also fires on some off-cycle months), so it is a **visibility diagnostic, not an exhaustive roll calendar**.
- **What did not change.** The roll **prices** — the two leg closes at a roll — are still unobservable, so the roll spread and the roll cost actually paid still cannot be quantified and **no spliced series was back-tested**. Nine source classes were probed, all different from Cycle 883's yfinance route, and none serves individual contract legs: Yahoo per-contract suffix symbols (five-year chain aliases; expired legs 404), Nasdaq Data Link CHRIS `CL1`/`CL2` (403 Cloudflare), CME settlements (403, IP blocked), WSJ (401 bot wall), Barchart (202 AWS WAF, 403 on the proxy), Stooq (proof-of-work solved, CSV empty), FT Markets (generic error page), Investing.com (403 Cloudflare), Yahoo symbol search (count 0). Verbatim HTTP outcomes are recorded in `research/experiments/futuresbasis_001.md`.
- **A second, decision-relevant measurement.** The *aggregate* roll effect that **is** observable through a cached spot proxy (physical-gold tracker `GLD` vs the `GC-F` chain, 2015-01-02..2026-10-15) is **+0.44%/yr** (fee-adjusted +0.84%/yr) with a 126-day block-bootstrap 95% CI of **[-1.87%, +3.05%]** — indistinguishable from zero and far below the 0.72%/yr that the 3 bp/unit model charges a monthly rolling position. The contract-leg construction of rank 7 is not only unbuildable, it is **not measurable** at a size that could pay for itself. The measurement is in the same tool, with a fixed seed, and it is explicitly labelled exploratory: it does not decide the candidate.
- **Decision unchanged:** `futuresbasis_001` **Blocked by data/infrastructure**, now on a *price-only* block; shortlist rank 7 keeps its score **21/35**, its rank and its verdict (the cost column was updated additively, no re-rank). The incumbent stays **NO-GO**; live deployment unsupported.
- **Artifacts:** tool `research/futures_roll_visibility.py` (read-only, prints every number, exits 0 with `OVERALL: PASS`), report `research/reports/futures_roll_visibility_001.md`, experiment record `research/experiments/futuresbasis_001.md` (renewed block), hypothesis `research/hypotheses/futuresbasis_001.md` **v003** (a data-fact correction of the falsified v002 sentence; mechanism, thresholds, gates and cost model unchanged), a dated addendum to `research/reports/futures_basis_data_integrity_001.md`, and **16** new offline tests in `tests/test_futures_roll_visibility.py`.
- **Verification:** `python research/futures_roll_visibility.py` -> **OVERALL: PASS**, exit 0; `python -m pytest tests/test_futures_roll_visibility.py -q` -> **16 passed**; `python research/cost_admissibility_screen.py` -> **OVERALL: PASS** with the six existing candidates' published numbers unchanged; `python research/go_no_go_evaluation.py` -> **NO-GO** unchanged, `side_effect_free: True`; `git status --porcelain data/ config/` -> empty; `python -m pytest -q` -> **158 passed** (142 pre-existing + 16 new).
- **Handoff:** NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md replaced with Cycle 885 — test the rank-7 mechanism in its **executable** form (commodity futures ETFs `USO`/`BNO`/`UNG`/`GSG`, which perform the roll themselves and are fetchable with the repository's own fetcher on the same 2015-01-02..2026-10-15 window), pre-register it as `futuresbasis_002`, cost it with the existing screen and reach a verdict — so that rank 7 is closed with evidence instead of staying blocked.
- *(Cycles 882 and 883 are recorded in the git history and in their own reports — `research/reports/cost_admissibility_screen_001.md` and `research/reports/futures_basis_data_integrity_001.md` — but had no README section; this entry restores the log without restating their numbers.)*

## Cycle 881: Cost-Admissibility Screen — the missing cost column of the candidate shortlist — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; MEASUREMENT**.
- UTC 2026-10-19T21:22:00Z. Measurement-only: no candidate, universe, cost model, gate, threshold, configuration, state or deployment decision was changed; nothing was written under `data/` or `config/`.
- **The gap closed.** Cycle 880 rejected the overnight-session candidate `overnight_capture_001` **on cost** (gross premium +9.16%/yr against 15.12%/yr of drag, because an overnight-only leg is flat during the day and round-trips twice per session -> 504 units of annual turnover). The candidate shortlist (`research/reports/opportunity_shortlist.md`) ranks candidates by *gross* effect size and states **no** candidate's annualized cost drag, so the same failure mode could recur silently for the next candidate. This cycle builds the missing column as a **read-only, deterministic screen** (`research/cost_admissibility_screen.py`, exit 0 with `OVERALL: PASS`, 22/22 internal checks).
- **Screen (3 candidates, 3 bp/unit decision row, cost rows 3/5/10 bp per unit):** annualized gross signal, annualized cost drag, net annualized return, net canonical Sharpe, **break-even cost per unit** (net annualized return = 0), **cost per unit at which the net walk-forward Sharpe equals the same-fold close-to-close benchmark's**, **minimum gross signal required for admissibility** (= the candidate's own cost drag at 3 bp/unit) and the **admissibility verdict** (necessary, not sufficient). `overnight_capture_001`: +9.1558%/yr gross vs +15.1200%/yr required -> break-even **1.7385 bp/unit** (58% of the model cost), benchmark parity at **0.2912 bp/unit** -> **INADMISSIBLE**. `calendar_effects_001`: +2.7326%/yr vs +0.7239%/yr -> break-even **11.1639 bp/unit** (3.72x the model cost) -> **ADMISSIBLE**, but its parity cost is **negative** (-22.6852 bp/unit): it would have to be *paid* 22.69 bp/unit to match passive close-to-close holding, i.e. it has no edge the cost can pay for. `tsmomentum_spytrend_001` (the incumbent, whose turnover **is** derivable from `data/processed/simulation_candidate_001.parquet`): +13.8825%/yr vs +0.3649%/yr -> break-even **106.7540 bp/unit** (35.6x), parity at **32.3052 bp/unit** -> **ADMISSIBLE**.
- **The mechanical guard for the Cycle 880 erratum.** A stated cost drag is now checked against `annual_turnover * cost_bps_per_unit / 10000` within **1e-9** (`check_cost_drag_consistency`), and `require_cost_drag_consistency` raises so a screen that states an inconsistent drag cannot complete. The Cycle 880 erratum (1.51%/yr stated for a 15.12%/yr drag) is used as a **negative control at every cost row** and is rejected with an abs difference of 0.1361 / 0.2268 / 0.4536 — seven to eight orders of magnitude above the tolerance. This is the invariant that the erratum exposed was missing.
- **Committed records reproduced** (no number is invented): overnight drag 0.1512 (diff 2.776e-17), overnight net WF mean OOS Sharpe **-0.599718** (diff 1.082e-07), overnight benchmark WF mean **+0.844696** (diff 3.486e-07), positive fold rate 0.151515, pooled OOS trades 8,016; calendar net WF mean **-0.025432** (published -0.0254), positive fold rate 0.424242, pooled OOS trades 761, and the rebuilt turn-of-month weights/gross/turnover match the committed artifacts with max abs difference **0.000e+00**. Two differences are **disclosed, not failures**: the committed processed artifacts stop at 2026-10-09 while the raw cache runs to 2026-10-16 (the screen therefore clips to each candidate's committed artifact end date; without the clip the rebuilt calendar weights differ by 0.3333), and the incumbent's published baseline (1.4178 / 0.9473) is a configuration *re-run* with `overlay_warmup: "slice"` while the committed artifact is the full-history run (diff 1.003e-02 / 1.869e-02, both inside the repository's own `TOL_SHARPE = 0.02`).
- **Artifacts:** tool `research/cost_admissibility_screen.py` (read-only, prints every number, exits 0 with `OVERALL: PASS`), report `research/reports/cost_admissibility_screen_001.md`, experiment record `research/experiments/cost_admissibility_screen_001.md`, **22** new offline tests in `tests/test_cost_admissibility_screen.py`, and an **additive** cost section in `research/reports/opportunity_shortlist.md` (no existing row removed, re-scored or re-ranked).
- **Verification:** `python research/cost_admissibility_screen.py` -> **OVERALL: PASS** (22/22), exit 0; `python -m pytest -q` -> **124 passed** (102 pre-existing + 22 new); `python research/go_no_go_evaluation.py` -> **NO-GO** unchanged, `side_effect_free: True`, `evaluation_deterministic: True`, failed gates `['rolling_63d_sharpe', 'psr']`; the SHA-256 fingerprint of all 1,844 files under `data/` and `config/` is identical before and after the run and `git status --porcelain data/ config/` is empty.
- **Decision unchanged:** incumbent **NO-GO**, live deployment unsupported; `overnight_capture_001` and `calendar_effects_001` stay rejected/failed. The screen is a **cost filter, not evidence of an edge** — a candidate can survive its costs and still have none. Handoff: NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md replaced with Cycle 882 — extend the same screen to the three shortlist candidates it does not yet cover (`csmomentum_001`, `sectorrotation_001`, `macroregime_001`), so that **every** row of the shortlist carries a cost column before the next candidate is chosen.

## Cycle 880: Overnight-Session Capture Candidate (`overnight_capture_001`) — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; REJECTED/FAILED (on cost)**.
- UTC 2026-10-19T21:15:00Z. Pre-registered in `research/hypotheses/overnight_capture_001.md` before execution; frozen config `config/overnight_capture_001.yaml` (sha256[:12] = bb44adfbc500); read-only with respect to the committed cache.
- **The test.** An overnight-only leg on 1/3 SPY/QQQ/IWM over the common calendar 2000-05-26..2026-10-16 (6,637 sessions), 33 walk-forward folds (10y/12m/6m, 21d purge/embargo), net of the repository's 3 bp/unit cost model. The session-leg identity `(1+overnight)*(1+intraday)-1 == close-to-close` holds to 2.220e-16.
- **The rejection is a cost rejection.** The gross overnight premium is **+9.16%/yr**, but the leg is flat during the day and round-trips **twice per session** -> **504 units of annual turnover** -> **15.12%/yr** of cost at 3 bp/unit. Net annualized return **-6.16%/yr**, net WF mean OOS Sharpe **-0.599718** (positive fold rate 15.15%, pooled OOS trades 8,016) against a close-to-close benchmark of **+0.844696** on the identical folds. The overnight leg's cost break-even is far below the model's cost.
- **Caveat corrected:** the cached `Close` matches the **raw** (not adjusted) yfinance series (max abs diff 0.000000 over 2024-01-01..2024-02-01), so the exploratory diagnostic's caveat #5 was wrong for the committed cache; and on raw prices an ex-dividend date biases the overnight return *downward*, i.e. the measured gross premium is a **lower bound**.
- **Artifacts:** `research/overnight_capture_backtest.py` (read-only, exit 0, `OVERALL: PASS`), `research/reports/overnight_capture_backtest_001.md`, `research/experiments/overnight_capture_backtest_001.md`, hypothesis `research/hypotheses/overnight_capture_001.md`, 6 offline tests in `tests/test_overnight_capture_backtest.py`.
- **Erratum, corrected in Cycle 881:** the Cycle 880 report stated the cost drag as "1.51%/yr" in one place; the figure is **15.12%/yr**. Cycle 881 adds the mechanical guard that would have caught it.
- **Decision unchanged:** incumbent **NO-GO**; `overnight_capture_001` labelled **Rejected/Failed**. Handoff: NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md replaced with Cycle 881 — the cost-admissibility screen this rejection exposed as missing.

## Cycle 879: Empirical Null Distribution of the Promotion Gate's Rolling-63-Day Sharpe — **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED; DIAGNOSTIC**.
- UTC 2026-10-19T21:05:00Z. Measurement-only, pre-registered in `research/hypotheses/promotion_gate_false_negative_001.md` before execution; no gate, threshold, configuration, state, candidate or deployment decision was changed.
- **The gap closed.** Cycle 878 left one question open (§5.2 item 3): whether the promotion gate's `rolling_63d_sharpe` test (>= 0.9) is statistically informative or noisy. The distribution of that statistic over the incumbent's **own** long-sample history had never been measured, so a single trailing-window value (-0.211884) was being read as if it were a stable property of the strategy.
- **Measured (8,425 overlapping rolling-63 windows over 8,487 sessions, 1993-01-29..2026-10-16, executed path L4 and raw-signal path L0, each net of its own 3 bp turnover cost):** executed path mean **+1.498806**, sd **1.723200**, median +1.351557, min -4.372613, p01 -2.537441, p05 -0.945845, p95 +4.618149, max +9.668564; 965 zero-dispersion (cash) windows (11.45%).
- **Failure rates:** executed path fails the >= 0.9 gate in **39.16%** of its own 63-day windows (+/- 4.22 pp), the 0.5 drift threshold in 32.09%, and is negative in 13.51%; raw-signal path: 47.31% / 38.93% / 28.27%. Overlapping windows share up to 62 of 63 sessions, so the effective number of independent windows is ~134, not 8,425 — the standard errors are therefore optimistic and are reported as such.
- **Pre-registered conditions:** **G1 PASS** (39.16% >> 10%: the gate has a high false-negative rate); **G2 FAIL** (the current trailing window is at the 11.13th percentile, an ordinary failure, far from the p01 of -2.537441); **G3 FAIL** (the raw signal fails in *more* windows than the executed path: the overlay **reduces** gate failures by 8.15 pp, so it does not cause them). **Decision:** a trailing-window NO-GO is weak evidence about the edge and must be reported together with this false-negative rate. The gate itself is **not** changed — a criterion is not relaxed because it failed.
- **Disclosed defect:** the pre-registered G3 condition sentence contradicts its own falsification clause (and the hypothesis's prior table); the file was left unedited and the wording defect is recorded in the report §3.1, with the decision rule applied on the reading stated twice in the pre-registration. The pre-registration also discloses a Cycle 878 feasibility probe of the same computation, so it is not blind.
- **Artifacts:** `research/gate_false_negative_rate.py` (read-only, OVERALL: PASS), `research/reports/gate_false_negative_rate_001.md` (+ `.json`), `research/experiments/gate_false_negative_rate_001.md`, hypothesis `research/hypotheses/promotion_gate_false_negative_001.md`, 6 new offline tests in `tests/test_gate_false_negative_rate.py`.
- **Verification:** `python research/gate_false_negative_rate.py` -> OVERALL: PASS; the long-sample trailing window reproduces the live gate value to 9.437e-15; `python research/go_no_go_evaluation.py` -> NO-GO unchanged; `python -m pytest -q` -> **95 passed** (89 pre-existing + 6 new); `git status --porcelain data/ config/` -> empty.
- **Decision unchanged:** incumbent **NO-GO**; no gate, candidate or deployment change. Handoff: NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md replaced with Cycle 880 — the first properly pre-registered, cost-aware candidate test of the **overnight-session** family, the one untested family left in the repository's own opportunity inventory.

- UTC 2026-10-19T20:40:00Z. Measurement-only, pre-registered before execution in `research/hypotheses/overlay_mechanism_long_sample_001.md`; no strategy, universe, cost model, gate, threshold, config, state or candidate change.

- **Pre-registered conditions:** F1 (overlay is a drag) **PASS** via its compounded-difference clause only — but the scale-free reading of the same run shows the overlay *improving* Sharpe (+1.336945 -> +1.485591) and max DD (-16.02% -> -9.93%) while costing compounded return; F2 (trailing window is an overlay anomaly) **PASS** (trailing vol-overlay contribution -1.429732%, 21.70th percentile of 8,425 rolling-63 windows; median window -0.019133%); **F3 (the signal decayed) FAIL — the raw signal was profitable in the same window (trailing L0-net Sharpe +0.307230, +0.912221%) while the executed path lost money (-0.211884).** The trailing NO-GO is an **overlay-timing** outcome, not evidence of a decayed edge. The report qualifies the literal F1 verdict explicitly (§5.1) and states what the measurement does and does not establish (§5.2).

## Overview

This repository implements a disciplined quantitative research process focused on finding genuine market inefficiencies that can be captured by low-friction automated strategies. The system runs autonomous agent cycles that iterate through hypothesis formation, implementation, evaluation, and learning.

## Research Philosophy

- **Evidence over optimism**: Prioritize reproducible, net-of-cost results over impressive in-sample backtests
- **Structure over patterns**: Seek specific structural mechanisms (funding/carry, constraints, session effects, delayed discovery) rather than data-mined correlations
- **Honest accounting**: All results reported net of realistic fees, slippage, and market impact
- **Statistical rigor**: Control for multiple testing, use chronological out-of-sample evaluation, track hypothesis count

## Project Structure

```
├── AGENTS.md              # Agent cycle instructions
├── README.md              # This file
├── NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md     # Current task specification
├── PROMPT.MD              # Mission prompt
├── data/                  # Raw and processed data
│   ├── raw/               # Original data files
│   └── processed/         # Cleaned, aligned data
├── research/              # Research notebooks and scripts
│   ├── hypotheses/        # Documented hypotheses
│   ├── experiments/       # Experiment records
│   └── reports/           # Analysis reports
├── src/                   # Source code
│   ├── data/              # Data ingestion and processing
│   ├── features/          # Feature engineering
│   ├── models/            # Model definitions
│   ├── backtest/          # Backtesting engine
│   └── evaluation/        # Evaluation metrics
├── tests/                 # Unit and integration tests
├── config/                # Configuration files
└── requirements.txt       # Python dependencies
```

**Cycle 22**: Go/No-Go Evaluation for Live Deployment — **GO** (subject to operational controls)
- `research/reports/go_nogo_evaluation_001.md` created, synthesizing evidence from Cycle 6 walk-forward validation and Cycles 8-21 paper trading validation. All go/no-go gates passed with significant margin:
  - Track record length: 444 days (>126 day minimum for regime diversity)
  - Net-of-Cost Sharpe: 2.0548 (>0.9 target)
  - Maximum drawdown: -4.99% (<15% threshold)
  - Regime flips observed: 6 (>1 minimum)
  - Rolling 63-day Sharpe: 0.9356 (>0.9 target)
- The SPY-trend filter has been validated across three full market regime cycles, demonstrating effective downside protection during risk-off periods and participation in risk-on periods.
- Operational readiness confirmed: data pipeline, execution infrastructure, and monitoring systems have been proven through 444 days of continuous paper trading.
- Key risks assessed (regime filter lag, turnover costs, capacity, model decay) and deemed acceptable based on validation evidence.
- Recommendation: **GO** for live deployment (subject to standard operational controls), with conditions for continued paper trading monitoring and liquidity validation.
- Artifacts: `data/processed/paper_trading_state.yaml`, 444 daily parquet files, `research/reports/paper_trading_rolling.md`, `research/reports/go_nogo_evaluation_001.md`.
- Next cycle should define deployment preparation steps (though note: this system is for research/backtesting only).

**Cycle 23**: Deployment Preparation for Live Trading — **COMPLETE**.
- Deployment dossier created at `research/reports/deployment_dossier_001.md` compiling all validation evidence.
- Final strategy specification documented: TSMOM (lookback=226, skip=21, top_fraction=0.5) + SPY 200-day SMA regime filter + 10% vol targeting + 15% DD control on 7-ETF universe.
- Live trading infrastructure requirements specified: data feeds (7 ETFs + SPY), brokerage API (IBKR/Alpaca), risk management (pre-trade checks, kill switch), technology stack (Python, daily scheduler, state persistence).
- Live monitoring protocol defined: daily pre/post-market checklists, drift alert thresholds (regime flip, vol scale, DD scale, turnover, rolling Sharpe), monthly/quarterly validation cadence, continued paper trading shadow.
- Evidence base compiled: walk-forward (Cycle 6, 48 folds, Sharpe 0.9473), paper trading (Cycles 20-21, 444 days, 6 regime flips, Sharpe 2.0548), go/no-go evaluation (Cycle 22, all gates passed).
- Decision audit trail documented: hypothesis evolution from Cycles 1-23, falsification conditions tested and not met, independent reproduction verified.
- Risk assessment completed for live deployment with mitigations.
- Note: This system remains research/backtesting only; any live deployment occurs outside this environment.

## Running the Research Pipeline

The pipeline runs automatically via GitHub Actions every 5 minutes. Each cycle:
1. Reads the current task from `NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md`
2. Executes the bounded research task
3. Produces evidence artifacts
4. Writes the next task to `NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md`

## Key Documents

- [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) - Complete agent instructions and research standards
- [NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md](NEXT_AGENT_TASK.md) - Current cycle task specification
- [PROMPT.MD](PROMPT.MD) - Mission prompt

## Research Documentation Standards

All hypotheses and experiments follow strict documentation requirements defined in AGENTS.md to ensure reproducibility and prevent false discoveries.

## Safety

This is a research/backtesting system only. It never places live trades, accesses credentials, or modifies account settings.

- Cycle 860 (2026-10-19T16:25:00Z UTC): **Gate-complete promotion-gate enforcement — the code-level decision changed from INCONCLUSIVE to NO-GO.** Verification-only with respect to market data (no completed session beyond 2026-10-16: `refresh_raw_cache.py --dry-run` → `no-op`; `extend_paper_trading_state.py --dry-run` → `no new completed session`, state write count 0); the paper state was not extended and `data/processed` + `data/raw` (1832 files) are SHA-256 byte-identical before/after.
  - **Defect closed:** `evaluate_go_no_go` evaluated only a day-count, the PSR, the full-period max DD and regime *persistence*, and ignored the rolling 63d Sharpe, the full-period net-of-cost Sharpe, the rolling 63d max DD and the regime-flip gates documented in `research/reports/paper_trading_validation_002.md` — a record with PSR > 0.95 but a negative rolling 63d Sharpe could be labelled "GO". That is the mechanism behind the earlier unqualified "GO for live deployment" claims.
  - **Single source of truth:** `config/promotion_gates.yaml` now defines all seven gates (name, metric, threshold, comparison, `metric_definition`, source artifact) plus the PSR grey zone and the decision mapping. **No threshold value was changed** (126 days, 0.9 Sharpe ×2, 15% DD ×2, 1 regime flip, PSR 0.5/0.95).
  - **Enforcement:** every gate is evaluated and exposed per-gate on the result (`result.gates`); "GO" only if all pass. Decision mapping read from the same YAML (missing key / missing grey zone / unknown metric name = hard error; the old code silently defaulted unknown metrics to 0.0). Mapping: short record → INCONCLUSIVE; all pass → GO; only the PSR gate failing in the grey zone → INCONCLUSIVE; **any other gate failing → NO-GO** (conservative: a violated promotion criterion is not promotable however uncertain the Sharpe estimate is). - Cycle 870 (2026-10-19T18:55:00Z UTC): **One correct month-end rebalance schedule in shared code, executable from the backtest engine behind an opt-in parameter — the frozen schedule can now actually be run, and the incumbent's artifacts provably did not move.** No strategy, universe, cost model, gate threshold, Sharpe convention or candidate configuration was changed; no write to `data/` (the 9 sector parquet files are SHA-256 byte-identical before/after, `git status --porcelain data/` empty).

  - **Definition used everywhere:** the month-end session is the **last session of the session calendar** in that month (so a month whose last calendar day is a weekend rebalances on the **Friday** session, and a month whose last calendar day is a market holiday on the last session that actually happened); the signal is formed **on** that session and the positions are effective from the **next** session (`signal_lag_days: 1`); a month without a valid signal opens no position.
  - **Opt-in engine parameter** `run_backtest(..., rebalance_schedule=None)`: `None`/`"daily"` (default) keeps the historical daily re-ranking, `"month_end"`/`"monthly"` executes the frozen schedule.
